#7312. Security Engineer, Vulnerability Management at LiveKit

LiveKit is building the infrastructure layer for the voice-driven era of computing. Our platform gives developers everything they need to build, test, deploy, scale, and observe agents in production. Founded in 2021, LiveKit powers voice AI applications for OpenAI, xAI, Salesforce, Coursera, Spotify, and thousands of others, collectively facilitating billions of calls each year.

Our customers trust us with audio, video and data belonging to their own end users, so a security failure on our side puts their data and their business at risk. We are hiring a Security Engineer to build and run our vulnerability management and security operations program. Vulnerability management is a core capability for keeping our security posture strong as the platform and the company grow: knowing what we run, finding what is exposed, and getting the right fixes shipped quickly. It also underpins our customer commitments and the compliance programs we are building. Our program is early, and you will shape it. You will join a small Trust team that covers security, IT and compliance, and you will own this program end to end from your first week.

think like both a builder and a breaker, and understand security from first principles

take an automation-first and pragmatic approach to risk mitigation

translate security concerns into engineering action

can clearly explain complex technical concepts to others

are a fast learner and thrive in ambiguity

Select, deploy and operationalize scanning tooling across our production assets.

Run vulnerability management end to end: triage, severity in our environment, owner assignment, remediation SLAs and reporting.

Build engineering-first triage and remediation workflows that fit how our engineering teams already work, and automate the routing and follow-up.

Build a remediation dashboard that engineering and leadership read and act on.

Automate collection of the vulnerability management evidence our compliance programs depend on, in partnership with our Compliance team.

Build automation, including agentic tooling, that removes manual security operations work and keeps pace with engineering growth.

5+ years in security engineering, security operations or software engineering, with a significant share of that focused on security.

Hands-on experience building or running a vulnerability management program or software security tooling (SCA, container, cloud or host scanning).

An engineering-first approach to triage and remediation: you get findings fixed by making the fix easy to ship.

The judgment to set a finding's real severity in our environment, using CVSS as one input.

Strong scripting and automation skills in a language such as Go or Python, and comfort reading infrastructure as code such as Terraform.

A pragmatic approach to risk: you can explain why a finding matters to the business and how fast it needs to be fixed.

Experience with real-time media, networking or WebRTC systems.

Experience supporting compliance audits such as HIPAA, PCI-DSS, ISO 27001 or SOC 2.

Incident response or application security experience.

Experience in AI security, such as securing LLM applications or agent infrastructure.

An opportunity to build something truly impactful to the world

Contribute to open source alongside world-class engineers

LiveKit is an equal opportunity employer and does not discriminate on the basis of any characteristic protected by applicable law. If you require a reasonable accommodation during the application or interview process, please contact recruiting@livekit.io.

#7298. Director of Technology Partnerships at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We’re looking for a Director of Technology Partnerships to lead the teams responsible for growing Supabase through technology integrations and our marketplace ecosystem.

You’ll bring together two complementary motions: partnerships that embed Supabase into the products developers already use, and a marketplace that helps developers discover and connect complementary technologies to their Supabase projects. Your job is to turn both into durable sources of revenue, adoption, and customer value.

Reporting to the Head of Partnerships, you’ll lead and develop the Integrations and Marketplace teams, set their commercial strategy, and work hands-on with our most important partners. A central part of the role is working closely with AI labs to build strategic relationships, develop integrations, and bring joint opportunities to market. You’ll partner closely with Product, Engineering, Sales, Marketing, and Finance to take integrations from idea to launch—and grow their impact long after launch.

This role is based in the San Francisco Bay Area, where you’ll build relationships with leading AI, developer tools, and infrastructure companies. Supabase is remote-first, and you’ll work across a globally distributed team.

Lead, coach, and develop the Integrations and Marketplace partnerships teams, including team leads and partner managers.

Set a clear strategy for where Supabase invests across strategic technology partnerships, marketplace categories, and emerging ecosystem opportunities.

Define team objectives, partner coverage, investment priorities, and operating cadence. Make thoughtful tradeoffs between high-touch strategic partnerships and scalable programs.

Build a culture of ownership, commercial discipline, and strong cross-functional execution.

Drive revenue from technology integrations through partner-driven customer acquisition, product consumption, expansion, retention, co-sell opportunities, and commercial arrangements.

Own the commercial performance of the technology partnerships and marketplace portfolio, including forecasting and reporting on progress, risks, and return on investment.

Build and deepen relationships with AI labs, working closely with their product, engineering, business development, and executive teams to identify and execute high-impact partnership opportunities.

Develop executive relationships with strategic partners across developer tools, cloud, and adjacent infrastructure.

Lead or support negotiations for complex partnerships, including integration, referral, revenue-sharing, and joint go-to-market agreements.

Help partner managers build joint business plans that connect partner priorities to measurable outcomes for Supabase and its customers.

Lead and develop the Marketplace team, setting its commercial and partner strategy and holding the team accountable for outcomes.

Guide the team in identifying the capabilities developers need, recruiting the right partners, and helping those partners launch and grow.

Set priorities and allocate resources across strategic, high-touch partners and scalable paths for emerging and self-serve partners.

Ensure the team works effectively with Product and Engineering on integration quality, partner onboarding, discovery, activation, and the developer experience across product surfaces.

Enable the team to work with Marketing and Sales to launch integrations, enable teams, and drive ongoing adoption—not just announcements.

Establish clear measures for partner-sourced and influenced pipeline, integration adoption, revenue, consumption, retention, and marketplace performance.

Work with Finance, Data, and Partner Operations to improve attribution, reporting, forecasting, and partner revenue processes.

Represent the voice of partners and customers in product and go-to-market planning, while aligning stakeholders around shared priorities.

Identify risks early, remove blockers, and communicate decisions and outcomes clearly to leadership.

Have significant experience in technology partnerships, business development, or ecosystem leadership at a developer tools, cloud, infrastructure, or SaaS company.

Have led and developed partner-facing teams, including managers or senior individual contributors, and can balance strategic direction with hands-on execution.

Have a track record of building partnerships that produced measurable revenue, product adoption, or customer growth.

Have negotiated complex commercial agreements and understand partnership economics, including revenue share, referrals, and consumption-based models.

Have worked closely with Product and Engineering to ship and grow technical integrations. You can engage credibly with technical teams, including those at AI labs, even if you aren’t the person writing the code.

Know how to build relationships with executives while earning the trust of product, engineering, and go-to-market teams.

Use data to prioritize opportunities, assess partner performance, and make decisions when attribution is imperfect.

Are comfortable building in an evolving environment, communicating in writing, and working asynchronously across time zones.

Built or scaled a developer marketplace, integration ecosystem, or partner platform.

Worked with AI LLMs, AI app builders, databases, open-source products, or developer infrastructure.

A background in software development, with an understanding of how developers build and integrate products.

Experience growing both a small number of strategic partnerships and a broader, scalable partner program.

A strong network in the San Francisco Bay Area technology ecosystem.

#7296. The Art, Science and Theater of Modern Marketing ft. Thomas Ranese

What if the best marketing advantage you have is something that cannot be replicated by an algorithm?

For Thomas Ranese, CMO of Intuit, it starts with theater, empathy and understanding what people are actually trying to achieve.

In this episode of Marketing Vanguard, Jenny Rooney sits down with Thomas to unpack how an unconventional career spanning theater, Google and Uber shaped his approach to marketing leadership.

From growing up behind the counter of his father’s pasta shop to leading marketing at one of the world’s biggest financial technology platforms, Thomas gets into why emotional connection can be just as powerful as performance data.

He also addresses why AI needs human expertise to earn customer trust, and why brands should bring more humanity, creativity, and purpose into modern marketing.

Thomas Ranese is Chief Marketing Officer at Intuit, a global technology platform serving millions of customers worldwide with TurboTax, QuickBooks, Credit Karma and Mailchimp.

His career has taken him through some very different worlds, including theater, consulting, government and nonprofit work, Google and Uber. His approach to marketing is rooted in a simple idea: understand what people are trying to achieve, then build brands and experiences that help them get there. He strongly believes in storytelling and using humor to make complex information more relatable.

At Intuit, Thomas is helping shape the company’s evolution into an integrated, AI-powered financial technology platform, with a focus on combining technology with human expertise to help consumers and businesses prosper.

07:18 Marketing Is Theater, and It Goes Beyond Advertising — Thomas breaks down the three different kinds of theater that shape modern marketing: the storytelling and drama of advertising, the emotions and interactions built into customer experience, and the immersive experiences brands create through retail. He also believes real-world human interactions will become increasingly important as more of our lives move into digital and AI-powered environments.

12:44 What a Pasta Shop Taught Him About Customer Obsession — Thomas traces his interest in marketing back to his childhood, when he spent time working in his father’s small pasta shop. He loved being behind the counter, helping customers and figuring out what they needed. He even created his father’s first promotional newsletter on a Commodore 64. Looking back, he sees those early experiences as the foundation of his approach to customer connection, communication and service.

16:01 Why AI Needs Human Intelligence Too — Intuit is building AI into the core of its financial technology platform, but Thomas is particularly focused on the role human experts play alongside it. When customers are making important decisions about taxes, credit or their businesses, getting the right answer matters. AI can provide powerful technology and efficiency, but human expertise brings verification, empathy and the confidence customers need to act.

19:57 Money Is a Means to an End. So Is Your Product — Thomas challenges brands to think beyond what their products do and focus on what customers want to achieve with them. Small business owners aren’t necessarily starting businesses because they want to make money; they’re pursuing a craft, building a lifestyle, serving customers or creating opportunities for their families. The same principle applies across categories: brands become more meaningful when they understand the bigger ambitions their products help people achieve.

22:00 Intuit’s Goal for Financial Literacy — Thomas explains how Intuit is connecting its mission to power prosperity with financial literacy education. Through Intuit for Education, the company aims to reach 50 million students by 2030, addressing the fact that only 32% of students graduate able to pass a basic financial literacy exam. For Thomas, this is an opportunity to create meaningful social impact while building trust and introducing a new generation to the tools that can help them succeed.

26:02 Why CMOs Should be Fully Involved — Thomas pushes back on the idea that senior marketing leaders should step away from execution as their organizations grow. With technology and AI changing marketing so quickly, he believes CMOs need to stay hands-on, understand the tools and continue learning. He argues that the bigger the organization, the more important this becomes, especially when it is easy to become disconnected from the actual work and the customers being served.

Jenny Rooney is Chief Brand and Community Officer, leading strategy for the overall ADWEEK brand as well as the ways in which we serve and support our audiences with high-value content, products, partnerships and experiences, notably through our community programs such as Marketing Vanguard.

Select ADWEEK as one of your favorite sources and stay up to date on our latest content.

Plus, why fragmentation is getting in the way of better consumer experiences.

From an official day off work from Logitech to the “Spice City” sandwich from Popeyes, brands are nudging their way into the conversation.

#7294. Technical Account Manager (APJ) at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We’re seeking a Technical Account Manager (TAM) to be the primary technical partner for our Team & customers after the sale, helping them adopt, operate, and get long-term value from Supabase.

At Supabase, TAMs are the technical owners of their assigned accounts, working closely with our most valuable customers to:

Understand each customer’s architecture, use cases, constraints, and goals.

Validate deployments and guide customers through onboarding and early success.

Proactively surface risks and optimization opportunities before they become incidents.

Coordinate internal teams on complex escalations and drive root cause analysis.

Align Supabase capabilities with the customer’s roadmap.

Successful TAMs are proactive, technically credible, and customer-first. TAM is a relationship, not a response channel: you’ll act as an extension of your customers’ teams and advocate on their behalf within Supabase.

You’ll be joining our Success team, where you will be expected to lead and innovate around the following areas of responsibility:

Act as the technical owner for your assigned accounts, documenting environments, mapping dependencies, and tracking technical risks.

Validate architecture and deployment plans after the sale, guide initial configuration and best practices, and make sure customers understand platform capabilities and limitations.

Help customers reach early success milestones to shorten time to value and reduce early friction.

Regularly review customer environments and usage patterns to identify scalability, performance, reliability, security, and compliance risks and opportunities.

Plan upgrades and lifecycle events, and run architecture reviews for upcoming initiatives, including capacity and growth planning, feature readiness, and early identification of technical blockers.

Coordinate across Support and Engineering on complex or high-impact issues. TAMs are not frontline support, but provide the context, prioritization, and follow-through that drive resolution, root cause analysis, and preventative action.

Serve as the voice of the customer to Engineering and Product, sharing specific, reproducible feedback on recurring pain points and taking part in beta and early access programs.

Partner with Sales and Customer Success on account health and retention, with technical input on renewals, expansions, and evaluations, and early flags on risks to satisfaction.

6+ years of relevant work experience in engineering, SRE, technical consulting, or similar, including operating production systems under real constraints.

Strong technical depth across database internals, scalability, security posture, and infrastructure tradeoffs. You understand a customer’s architecture as well as they do, without escalating every question.

A proactive mindset: you spot risks and optimization opportunities before the customer does, and you treat your accounts like a personal portfolio.

Sound escalation judgment: you know when to escalate, when to resolve internally, and how to communicate clearly under pressure without creating noise.

Very strong communication skills. TAMs explain complex issues to non-technical executives and root causes to senior engineers, often in the same day.

Awareness of how TAM performance shows up in retention and NRR, including surfacing expansion signals and renewal risks early.

Experience working in async or globally distributed teams.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.

#7291. GTM, Strategic Planning at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search — all deeply integrated and designed for growth. We're building the best developer platform to power the next generation of software companies, and our go-to-market team is scaling fast to match.

We're looking for a lead in Go-To-Market Strategic Planning, to shape how Supabase's GTM engine runs. This is a high-leverage, high-ownership role that sits at the intersection of strategy, data, and execution. You'll work directly with Success, Sales, Marketing, Finance, Partnerships and Product to build the systems, processes, and insights that help our go-to-market teams move faster and smarter.

Own the end-to-end GTM Strategic Planning, covering territory design, segmentation design, target planning and GTM capacity planning.

Build and maintain a single source of truth for planning data across HubSpot, BigQuery and Hex.

Translate company priorities into field actions through designing the planning strategy and seeing it through to execution.

Partner with Sales Leadership to design and iterate on coverage models, segmentation, and team structures that reflect how developers actually buy.

Define and track the metrics that matter, and surface insights that drive action, not just reporting.

Lead the Strategic Planning roadmap: guide and lead in tooling investments and evaluate new systems and processes against real business needs.

Work cross-functionally with talent acquisition, ensuring GTM and territory models stay aligned.

Collaborate with GTM Engineering on automations and data pipelines so that your strategy translates into working systems, not just slide decks.

Operate with high ownership. You set your own agenda, escalate when needed, and don't wait to be asked.

Work asynchronously and document clearly. We're a globally distributed team and writing is how we think.

Bridge strategy and execution. From drafting segmentation approaches in Google Docs to using SQL to monitor its effectiveness.

Build for scale without over-engineering. Simple systems that work beat perfect systems that don't ship.

Stay close to the customer. Developers are our buyers, and understanding how they discover, adopt, and expand is core to everything you'll do.

Have 6+ years of experience in Revenue Operations, Strategy Consulting, GTM Strategy, or Sales Operations at a B2B SaaS or developer-tools company.

Are fluent in the GTM stack: HubSpot, BigQuery, and Hightouch.

Can write SQL comfortably and aren't afraid to get into the data yourself before asking an analyst.

Have experience designing or overhauling sales processes, forecasting cadences, or territory models in a high-growth environment.

Understand product-led growth motions: PLS, usage-based triggers, and how PLG data flows into commercial pipelines.

Thrive with autonomy and are energized by building things that didn't exist before you joined.

#7289. The Media RFP Is Quietly Becoming Obsolete ft. Bruce Williams

Media used to be one part of the marketing machine, but this approach became outdated a while ago.

In this episode of Marketing Vanguard, Jenny Rooney sits down with Bruce Williams, CEO of dentsu X North America, to examine how media is becoming the connective tissue across creative, commerce, CRM, data, and technology.

Bruce reveals why standalone media RFPs are disappearing, and what it really takes to design connected consumer experiences.

He also addresses why brands need to move from channel-first planning toward operating systems built around outcomes.

Bruce Williams is CEO of dentsu X North America. Here, his focus is increasingly on helping brands move beyond isolated media planning toward connected operating models that bring together data, content, technology and consumer experience.

With more than 20 years in media, Bruce has spent much of his career working at the intersection of media strategy, integrated marketing, technology and organizational transformation.

He also works with clients on AI-enabled transformation, agency interoperability and new ways to turn data into faster decision-making at both global and local levels.

01:26 The “Media RFP” Is Quietly Becoming Obsolete — Bruce says media RFPs rarely arrive as standalone asks anymore. Brands increasingly want integrated briefs that connect media with creative, experience, CRM and loyalty, or they want agencies to solve bigger transformation problems. So, the agency conversation has to shift from “Can you buy media well?” to “Can you help us operate better?”

03:13 Your Customer Doesn’t Experience Your Brand in Silos — Marketing may still be organized into separate teams, systems and channels, but the person receiving the experience does not care about those distinctions. Bruce argues that fragmentation remains one of the industry’s longest-running problems because disconnected structures eventually show up as disconnected consumer experiences.

06:22 On AI: Build the System, Then Stay Scrappy — Bruce does not see AI transformation as a choice between massive infrastructure projects and quick tactical wins. He points to work with Heineken that combines long-term data and technology integration with practical tools like local intelligence agents that move competitive and market data directly into local decisions.

08:51 Why Agencies Can’t Keep Saying “Our Way or Nothing” — For Bruce, the future of agency relationships comes down to interoperability and optionality. Closed systems create friction. Agencies need to work directly with brand data, technology and other partners instead of expecting clients to conform to one proprietary model. Openness is becoming part of the value proposition.

Jenny Rooney is Chief Brand and Community Officer, leading strategy for the overall ADWEEK brand as well as the ways in which we serve and support our audiences with high-value content, products, partnerships and experiences, notably through our community programs such as Marketing Vanguard.

Select ADWEEK as one of your favorite sources and stay up to date on our latest content.

The Intuit CMO on why the C-suite still needs to get their hands dirty.

From an official day off work from Logitech to the “Spice City” sandwich from Popeyes, brands are nudging their way into the conversation.

#7267. Senior Analytics Engineer at Kit

Kit is the email marketing platform experts choose to grow their audience, deepen their relationships, and build a business around the work they love. Our Sales team needs a dedicated data partner, and creator commerce (how creators monetize by selling digital products) is becoming a bigger part of how Kit and our creators scale. Kit is growing, so we're looking for a Senior Analytics Engineer to own Kit's sales and commerce data from end to end.

Accurate sales data gives our team a clear picture of the pipeline and the confidence to make informed decisions. That’s what makes this role so important.

Your primary focus will be Sales: the dbt models that turn our CRM data into trusted pipeline, deal, and funnel metrics, and the Omni dashboards our sales leader and Senior Revenue Operations Manager use to run the business. You'll also model creator commerce data, so we understand what creators sell and earn through Kit, and you'll pick up reporting work with our Product team, such as feature usage reporting. On a small data team, we step outside our usual lanes when the need is there, so the mix will shift over time.

You'll build for two kinds of consumers: people and AI. Teammates increasingly answer their own questions by querying our semantic layer through Omni's AI and MCP connections to AI tools. That means your models, metric definitions, and documentation need to be clear and correct enough for an AI agent to use them without a data teammate in the loop.

This is a full-time individual contributor role for someone who likes being close to the business, enjoys turning messy operational data into numbers people trust, and is happy to work on both the models and the dashboards that sit on top of them. When Sales and Product have clear, reliable data, we help more creators build more valuable businesses with Kit.

You'll report to Sam Umachi, Head of Data (Linkedin).

Sales and RevOps are your primary partners, who will rely on the models and reporting you own. You'll also partner with our Product team on reporting for features like Visual Automations and on how creators use Kit.

Within our data team, you'll work alongside a Staff Analytics Engineer who owns org-wide semantic layer governance and modeling standards, a Senior Data Engineer who owns ingestion and pipelines, and a Senior Business Analytics Partner focused on revenue opportunities. This role has no direct reports.

Get set up in our core tools: dbt Cloud, GitHub, Amazon Redshift, Omni, HubSpot, Linear, Hightouch and Slack. Set up your AI-assisted development environment and learn the shared AI workflows our data team uses. Open and merge your first small pull request so you learn our review and deployment flow early.

First Month: Map how HubSpot objects (deals, pipelines and stages, companies, contacts, and owners) flow into our dbt models, and document the gaps. Take over day-to-day ownership of the sales models and Omni dashboards that teammates have been covering. Audit current sales reporting and list where definitions differ or numbers don't reconcile. Get familiar with how commerce data (products creators sell and the revenue they earn through Kit) lands in the warehouse today. Agree with RevOps and Sales on the most important questions they need answered. Review teammates' pull requests and contribute to our shared SQL, dbt, and documentation standards. Share a written reflection with the team on what you've learned and where you'd focus first.

First Six Months: Ship and maintain well-tested, documented dbt models for core sales metrics, such as pipeline, deal velocity, stage conversion, win rates, and the sales-led funnel. Expose those metrics through the Omni semantic layer, working within the team's governance standards. Rebuild or refresh the core sales dashboards so pipeline reviews and forecasting conversations run on numbers that don't need manual reconciliation. Resolve competing sales metric definitions by writing down the options and tradeoffs, aligning with Sales and RevOps, and documenting the outcome. Model creator-level commerce data, such as products sold, revenue earned through Kit, and conversion, in partnership with data engineering. Write semantic layer descriptions, metric definitions, and documentation that let Omni's AI and MCP-connected AI tools answer routine sales questions correctly, and test them against real questions from Sales, RevOps and Product. Get Sales to the point where routine questions are answered through self-serve, whether in Omni dashboards, Omni's AI, or AI tools connected over MCP, rather than one-off requests.

Deep dbt experience. You build modular, tested, documented dbt projects, and you're comfortable with incremental models, snapshots, macros, CI, and scheduled jobs in dbt Cloud or similar.

#7249. Product Manager - Postgres at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're hiring a Product Manager to own Postgres itself at Supabase. Every Supabase project is a full Postgres database, and Postgres is the foundation that Auth, Storage, Realtime and Edge Functions run on. You'll own the product experience of the database: Postgres versions and upgrades, maintenance, observability, extensions and security.

You'll work closely with our Postgres engineering teams and with the PM lead for Databases, who leads Multigres. You'll define the Postgres requirements that architecture must meet, and own how customers experience their database through that transition.

This role is ideal for someone who loves Postgres, sweats the details that make a database trustworthy, and is excited about improving the experience for millions of developers. Small improvements here compound across every project on the platform.

You'll own Postgres versions, upgrades and end-of-life. Which major and minor versions we support, how and when customers upgrade, and how we retire old versions. Your goal is to make upgrades routine rather than risky.

You'll make maintenance predictable. You'll design how we schedule, communicate and run maintenance windows so customers stay informed and in control, and disruption is rare and expected.

You'll help developers understand their database. Query performance, metrics, logs and advisors: you'll own the path from "my database is slow" to the fix.

You'll own our extension ecosystem. Which extensions we offer, how we evaluate and support new ones, and how we keep them working across versions.

You'll own Postgres security. Roles and permissions, Row Level Security ergonomics, secure defaults, and how we respond to Postgres and extension CVEs.

You'll talk to developers across the full spectrum, from indie developers on their first project to enterprises running critical workloads, and bring what you learn into every prioritization call.

You'll own the problem definition, requirements and success metrics for what we ship, such as upgrade success rate, the share of the fleet on supported versions, and time-to-diagnose for performance problems. You'll set the metric before the team builds, then track it after launch.

You'll keep Engineering, Support and GTM aligned. You'll own the plan of record for what's coming, why, and in what order, and communicate version changes and deprecations clearly to customers.

4+ years of Product Management experience on databases, developer tools or infrastructure, or equivalent depth as an engineer, DBA or founder moving into product. You're comfortable commenting on an architecture doc and advocating for the user when trade-offs are required.

Know Postgres well, or another relational database well enough to ramp quickly. You're comfortable reading EXPLAIN output and talking about replication, WAL, roles and extensions.

Have shipped products where reliability and trust are the experience. You treat downtime and breaking changes as product problems, not just operational ones.

Write clearly. Your specs, announcements and deprecation notices leave little room for confusion.

Build with what you own. You run queries, test upgrades and try extensions yourself, and form opinions through first-hand use.

Use AI to move faster. You use AI tools to compress research, drafting and feedback synthesis so you spend more time on judgment calls.

Work async by default. Supabase is fully remote. You make decisions in writing and move work forward without meetings.

Ran Postgres in production: upgrades, tuning, incident response.

Worked on database observability, performance tooling, or security and compliance products.

Contributed to Postgres, its extensions, or the wider open-source community.

Curious about the kind of challenges you'll work on at Camunda? Watch this quick 30-minute talk from our engineers to learn more about the new Camunda Exporter and how we’re solving complex problems at scale

We are looking for a Product Builder to join the IDP & RPA pod. You will take real problems from customers, decide what is worth solving, and build and ship the solution yourself, using AI tools to get from idea to production quickly without lowering the bar.

Product Building is how Camunda's Experience organization builds the applications people use on top of Camunda 8, such as Hub, Operate, and ProcessOS. Small pods of builders own a product area from customer problem to shipped change, with no hand-offs between product management, design, and engineering. We are setting this model up now, so you will help shape how it works.

The IDP & RPA pod builds how Camunda processes work with documents and legacy applications. With intelligent document processing (IDP), customers create extraction and classification templates in Camunda Hub and publish them so a process can use AI to turn invoices and other documents into structured data. With robotic process automation (RPA), customers integrate legacy applications that have no APIs, using Robot Framework scripts on a standalone RPA worker. The hard problems come with putting LLMs into customer processes: extraction quality, model and prompt choices, testing and trust, and working for AI agents as well as for people.

Help shape product direction for IDP & RPA by combining customer input, usage data, and field feedback into clear priorities.

Partner with the other builders and product strategy to turn problems into shipped features, using short written proposals and prototypes to align early.

Make judgment calls with incomplete information within your scope and own the outcome.

Communicate trade-offs and decisions clearly to stakeholders who don't live in the details the way you do.

Use AI tools across the whole loop, from specs to prototypes to production-ready code, and hold a high bar for what you ship by reviewing and improving the output rather than accepting it.

Build, test, and ship improvements to the IDP & RPA experience, reading errors and tracing issues far enough to give your pod useful context.

Talk to customers and to support and customer success colleagues about how IDP is used and where it breaks, and turn what you learn into decisions about what to build next.

Pick up customer incidents and trace them across Hub, the connectors, and the deployed process of the customer.

Track what you ship against real outcomes and adjust when the data tells you to.

#7229. Information Security Engineer - Application Security at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. With built-in governance, auditability, and human oversight, Camunda gives enterprises the control they need to move AI from pilots to production — safely and at scale. Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

Hi there! We're hiring an Information Security Engineer with a focus on Application Security to join our Information Security team at Camunda.

We are a team that takes security seriously without making it feel like a locked door. We work in a transparent, straightforward way, and we care about doing the work right rather than just checking a compliance box.

You will work side by side with our R&D engineers: reviewing designs, threat modeling new features, digging into code and helping teams make good, risk-based decisions about the vulnerabilities that come in. You'll also help build the internal tools that make this work scale, including our CVE registry and the LLM-assisted workflows we use to analyse code and triage findings.

Camunda is an AI-first company, and InfoSec is no exception: AI agents are part of how we review code, assess vulnerabilities, and build our tools. You will help shape how that works.

This is a hands-on role for someone who already has solid AppSec foundations and wants to grow. We are a generalist team, so you will also take part in our on-call rotation and incident response, and get exposure to other areas of security. But most of your time will be spent with engineers and building and maintaining internal tools and processes.

Vulnerability management: triage findings from SCA, container and code scanning, external reports and internal security testing; assess whether Camunda is actually affected; agree fix / mitigate / accept with engineering and follow through to release.

Threat modeling and security reviews: run threat modeling sessions and design reviews with R&D teams for new features and architecture changes, and turn the results into concrete, prioritised actions.

Code analysis with LLMs: use and improve LLM-assisted tooling (agents, prompts, reusable skills) to review code, validate findings and reproduce issues, while applying your own judgement to know when the model is wrong.

Build internal security tooling: contribute to the CVE registry and related automation that connects scanners, engineering teams, and customer communication. Much of it is built on Camunda itself.

Help engineers do the secure thing by default: answer questions, review pull requests, improve guidelines and occasionally run a session or workshop.

On-call and incident response: share the InfoSec on-call rotation with the other engineers and help investigate and resolve security incidents.

Support the wider team: help write security advisories for our customers, answer customer security questions and support audits.

Around 3+ years in security or software engineering, with at least 2 of them working on application or product security (AppSec, product security, pentesting, or a security-focused developer role all count).

Experience at a SaaS or software product company, working closely with engineering teams that ship often through modern CI/CD and cloud environments.

Hands-on vulnerability triage: you've worked with tools like Snyk, Trivy, Semgrep, CodeQL or similar, can tell a real issue from a false positive, and can explain how exploitable something actually is in context (CVSS, exploitability, configuration).

Threat modeling and design review experience (e.g. STRIDE) on real systems, ideally web apps, APIs, or distributed services.

You read and write code. You're comfortable reading Java (Camunda is built on it) well enough to follow a data flow and spot a security bug. You can write scripts and small tools and contribute to an existing codebase with review.

You build with AI, not just chat with it. You use AI coding agents (Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot or similar) in your daily work to write tools, analyse code or automate tasks. And you know their output still needs a second look, especially when it comes to security.

Clear, friendly communication with engineers. You see yourself as an enabler, not a gatekeeper, and you can explain risk to people who aren't security specialists.

Some Python and/or JavaScript experience, or experience building internal tools.

Willingness to learn and use Camunda, since we build a lot of our own security automation on it. Prior BPMN / workflow experience is a plus.

Experience responding to security incidents or being part of an on-call rotation.

Exposure to security advisories, CVE disclosure or bug bounty programmes.

Knowledge of AI/LLM security risks (OWASP Top 10 for LLMs / agentic apps, prompt injection, tool abuse).

Experience securing Kubernetes or container-based workloads, or open-source / B2B software.

We offer competitive, fair, and transparent compensation. Salary ranges are location-based, with Standard and Major markets (global tech hubs) reflecting local competition.

The Annual Total Target Cash (base salary + 100% variable target, where applicable) shown below spans from the minimum in a Standard market to the maximum in a Major market. Final offers depend on skills, experience, and location, and we typically hire in the first half of the range to allow room for growth:

United States: $119,900.00 to $193,200.00

United Kingdom: £75,300.00 to £123,800.00

If you’re based elsewhere, you’ll be hired via Remote.com (our global employer partner), and your Talent Acquisition Partner will provide a personalized Total Rewards Calculator after your first interview.

Equity: We also offer equity (where applicable) through our Virtual Stock Option Plan (VSOP).

We invest in your wellbeing, growth, and ability to connect, along with perks that support you no matter where you’re based. Our benefits are globally designed and locally delivered where applicable.

Remote & Flexible: Work from anywhere with the setup that suits you, home office budget, co-working space support, and flexible time off to recharge when you need it.

In Person Connection: We invest in meaningful face time through our Annual Kickoff (Vienna in 2025, Madrid in 2026!), team offsites, and Camundi Connection Budgets, including contributing to meetups while travelling,, and local gatherings with fellow Camundi.

Health & Wellbeing: Access locally tailored healthcare, Modern Health for global mental wellbeing, and our Live Well Lifestyle Spending Account (LSA), a flexible, global benefit that puts you in control of your whole life, not just work, from: staying active, to caring for family, exploring personal passions, meaningful experiences, and investing in your financial wellbeing. The Live Well program launches in 2026 and scales to €1,000 annually from 2027.

Financial Security: Retirement and pension plans (often with company contributions), plus life and disability insurance where relevant.

Professional Growth: Up to $/€/£1,000 per year for self-driven learning: courses, certifications, books, you decide!

Because this part of our business is growing, we're looking for a Senior Product Marketing Manager, Scaling Creators to own how Kit wins, lands, and expands these businesses through a sales-assisted motion that sits alongside our self-serve product.

You'll report to Christina Lhi, Head of Marketing, and work alongside two other experienced Product Marketing Managers on Kit's product marketing team. Together, you'll keep the self-serve and sales-assisted stories feeling like one Kit.

Our sales leadership, on who we sell to, the deal toolkit, enablement, and win/loss

Account Executives and Solutions Consultants, on demo narratives, discovery, and technical and migration proof

Product, on packaging, launches, and roadmap input

RevOps and analytics, on how accounts get routed to sales, attribution, and pricing decisions

Creator Success, on case studies, customer references, and expansion plays

Demand gen, content, and events, on campaigns and events for established creators

You’ll own and lead our channel strategy, setting the vision, driving cross-functional alignment, and partnering with Marketing to bring it to life. You'll run a quarterly win/loss program and share what you learn openly, build a clear path for sales leads on our website, and make sure launches that matter to larger accounts ship with both a self-serve and a sales-assisted version. Your work has a direct impact on helping Kit reach $100,000,000 ARR while protecting the self-serve engine that got us here.

Segmentation and buyer definition: turning firmographic and product usage data into a clear picture of who to sell to and when

Messaging and positioning for a sales-assisted buyer, including competitive differentiation and objection handling

Sales enablement that reps actually use: demo narratives, discovery guides, business cases, and battlecards

Writing and storytelling that turns product depth into a business case a non-technical buyer can act on

Working with CRM, product analytics, and pipeline data to measure funnel conversion, win rate, and deal size

Customer and market/competitor research, including win/loss interviews and call analysis

Systems thinking: connecting people, processes, data, and tools to identify opportunities and build repeatable solutions across the go-to-market motion

Strong AI fluency: knowing when to leverage AI and how to build AI-powered workflows or tools that scale product marketing work, with human judgment and quality checks built in

Pricing and packaging input for a new tier or add-on (nice to have)

7+ years in B2B SaaS product marketing, including at least 2 years supporting a sales team selling to mid-market and/or larger accounts

Added a sales-led motion on top of a product-led one at a high-growth company, and can show what changed before and after with clear metrics

Wanting a sales playbook already in place. Much of this motion doesn't exist yet, and you'll be building it as you learn.

Preferring to be measured on output rather than outcomes. You'll share accountability with Sales for win rate, deal size, and expansion revenue.

Needing extensive approval before acting. You'll be trusted to make calls on speed versus quality and to explain your reasoning.

Discomfort with direct, candid feedback, especially from sellers on what's working in live deals.

We're 100% remote with team members across the globe. #7216. Attorney, New Verticals at Atticus

At any given time, 16 million Americans are experiencing a crisis that requires urgent help from our legal system or government. The right assistance could transform their lives. But today, most never get it.

Atticus makes it easy for any sick or injured person in crisis to get the life-changing aid they deserve. In the last six years, we've become the leading platform connecting people with disabilities to government benefits. We’re also quickly scaling an injury practice to help victims of accidents, misconduct, and violence get compensation from insurance.

So far, we've helped hundreds of thousands of people access over $10B in life-changing aid — and earned over 20,000+ five-star reviews. And we're just getting started.

We've raised more than $100 million from top VC firms like Forerunner, GV (Google Ventures), and True Ventures, with ambitions to create a category defining business assisting Americans in need.

Each month, tens of thousands of Americans seek our help for a single legal matter – applying for Social Security Disability, filing a Workers’ Compensation claim – but many of these clients are actually eligible for additional aid without ever knowing it.

Your job as Lead Attorney, New Verticals is to help us do right by these clients, helping them claim additional benefits through the social safety net. You’ll oversee the team of intake specialists who speak with clients who have secondary claims, evaluating the case facts and whether to connect them with an Atticus attorney or local attorney to provide legal advice and potentially take on their case.

This is a job that straddles the line between practicing the law and growing a business. It requires developing a strong understanding of multiple fields of law, and being able to accurately identify when we can help and when we cannot. But even more importantly, this job requires you to be an incredibly strong operator: you need to keep the big picture in mind and stay laser-focused on how we can achieve our goals, oftentimes finding outside-the-box ways to accomplish them. You will report to our General Manager of New Verticals and work closely with our CEO and Managing Attorney.

On any given day, you’ll spend your time:

Speaking with severely injured clients trying to rebuild their lives after a catastrophic accident

Sourcing and vetting local counsel in individual markets, holding a high bar for representation quality and ending relationships that fall below it

Working with our data and insights team to identify existing clients who may have unrecognized claims in adjacent fields of law, and setting the standard for which ancillary cases we take on

Building the playbook for claim types we haven't handled before, starting by screening cases yourself and eventually creating the protocol our intake specialists will run at scale

This is a rare opportunity for an attorney to join a profitable and growing public-interest law firm and take on one of our biggest priorities as a company.

You can understand the nuances of various contingency fields of law and can apply them to a variety of cases (including Social Security Disability, Workers’ Compensation, Personal Injury, and more) – you don’t need to be an expert today, but you should be a fast learner.

You can empathize with anyone and quickly build rapport and relationships while exercising good judgment, both with our clients and local counsel at law firms across the country.

You have strong instincts and judgment as an operator – you can run effective and efficient processes, and you don’t let them run you.

You have a demonstrated commitment to doing good and helping people.

You’ve built a track record as a great people manager – you know how to motivate talent and hold a high bar for output.

You have meaningful work experience outside of serving as a lawyer, ideally at a startup or other fast-paced, agile environment (e.g., a business operations team).

We are strongly committed to building a diverse team. If you're from a background that's underrepresented in tech, we'd love to meet you.

This role offers a unique chance to join a startup that has strong traction (substantial funding, well-respected backers, tremendous growth, and many happy customers) but is still small enough that you can have a huge impact and play a role in shaping our success.

We're a certified B Corporation tackling a critical social problem. Our mission to help people in need drives everything we do, and your work here will touch many lives.

We offer competitive pay — including equity — and generous benefits:

Medical and dental insurance with 100% of employees and their families premiums covered

15 vacation days & ~20 paid holidays each year (including two weeks at end-of-year)

$1,000 reimbursable stipend for education and training outside of work

Up to $1,200/year student loan repayment assistance

Humble, thoughtful, smart, fun colleagues

We anticipate the base salary band for this role will be between $140,000 and $175,000 in addition to equity and benefits. The salary at offer will be determined by a number of factors such as candidate's experience, knowledge, skills and abilities, as well as internal equity among our team.

This job is fully remote and we’re committed to empowering everyone with flexibility. #7207. Engineering Manager - Pipelines at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're looking for an Engineering Manager to lead the Pipelines team at Supabase. Supabase Pipelines is a managed change data capture product. It copies the tables you select from Supabase Postgres to other systems, then streams later changes. The product is in public alpha, with BigQuery, ClickHouse, DuckLake and Snowflake destinations.

Pipelines uses Postgres logical replication and Supabase ETL, our open source replication engine written in Rust. The team works close to the database. That means replication slots, WAL retention, initial table syncs, and the load they place on a customer's source database. The team also builds and runs the destination connectors. Correctness, ordering and recovery matter there as much as speed.

The Engineering Manager is a people, delivery and technical role. We don't expect you to write production code. We do expect you to join design discussions and help set the bar for engineering quality, testing and operations.

Build a healthy, high-trust team culture in a remote-first, globally distributed environment.

Run 1:1s, performance management, career development, and onboarding for your direct reports.

Handle people admin (leave, time off, etc.) for your team.

Own the hiring plan for your team and act as Hiring Manager. First round of interviewer for all roles on the team.

Partner with Talent Acquisition to source, interview, and close strong candidates.

Contribute to headcount planning, org design, and the evolution of engineering leadership at Supabase.

Own the team's roadmap and project management (Linear), driving accountability and clear milestones.

Manage workflow intake and Slack triage. These are the team’s primary inboxes for cross-team requests. You decide what gets routed where, prioritized, or pushed back on.

Own the team's weekly updates and keep stakeholders aligned without creating overhead.

Organize effort against support tickets and maintain the team's SLO.

Surface risks, dependencies, and tradeoffs early and clearly to leadership.

Train as an Incident Commander. Drive coordination, resolution, and post-incident follow-up.

Have 5+ years of engineering background, with enough depth to be credible with engineers building complex systems.

Have 3+ years managing remote engineering teams.

Have managed a team that built and ran data pipelines, change data capture, replication or streaming systems.

Have strong technical credibility in data systems. We look for delivery guarantees, ordering, idempotency, schema changes and backfills.

Know Postgres replication well, including logical replication, the WAL and replication slots, and how they affect a production database.

Know analytical destinations such as BigQuery, Snowflake, ClickHouse or lakehouse table formats like Iceberg.

Have led a team through production incidents on a system that holds customer data. You ran the follow-up work and kept the review blameless.

Have shipped a developer-facing data product to customers outside your company.

Have experience with Rust (not required but a plus)

Have managed remote, globally distributed teams that work asynchronously across disparate time zones.

Are technical enough to be credible with strong engineers, but genuinely passionate about people management. This is the work you want to do, not a track you're settling for.

Build psychological safety and a feedback culture. You give feedback often and in the moment, ask for it back, and create space for the team to do the same.

Operate as a multiplier. You help individuals achieve more than they could alone, and you don't need to be the best technical voice in the room.

Coach rather than direct. You remove blockers, connect people to context, and trust your engineers to own how the work gets done.

Have a real track record of performance management and can speak to specific examples of difficult conversations.

Are comfortable with ambiguity and can build structure where there isn't much.

Communicate clearly and write well. You default to async and written, set context early, and surface risks early.

Are collaborative, low-ego and adaptable. You thrive with high-autonomy senior engineers who don't need you to be the smartest person in the room.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

#7203. Engineering Manager - Realtime at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're looking for an Engineering Manager to lead the Realtime team at Supabase. Realtime lets developers send low-latency messages between clients, track and synchronize user state, and listen to database changes. Those three features are Broadcast, Presence and Postgres Changes. Broadcast also sends from the database and over HTTP, and clients can replay recent messages.

Realtime is a globally distributed Elixir cluster built on Phoenix. The team balances low latency, fair limits between projects, and the cost of running the service. Customers expect connections to stay up, so reliability matters as much as new features.

The Engineering Manager is a people, delivery and technical role. We don't expect you to write production code, but you you will lead a team of experienced engineers. We do expect you to join design discussions and help set the bar for engineering quality, testing and operations.

#7196. Teacher prep programs probed in Education Department’s latest anti-DEI push

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The agency said diversification efforts disregard “merit,” but critics say the programs level the playing field for historically excluded candidates.

The U.S. Department of Education opened a string of investigations into teacher preparation programs on Nov. 13, alleging that they discriminate on the basis of race under Title VI or sex under Title IX.

The investigations mark one of the first times the department's Office for Civil Rights has used civil rights protections to challenge diversification efforts in teacher scholarships, hiring, training and recruitment practices.

The programs at the center of the investigations are aimed at recruiting historically underrepresented groups in the teaching profession, such as men and teachers of color.

“Race, national origin, or sex should not decide who will shape America’s next generation, yet many states and universities seem to prioritize recruiting and training ‘minority’ candidates rather than evaluating candidates based solely on merit,” said Assistant Secretary for Civil Rights Kimberly Richey in a Nov. 13 statement.

“Education agencies and institutions that consider race, national origin, or sex as a means to withhold opportunities in selecting, supporting, and preparing teaching candidates violate federal law and compromise education excellence."

Richey concluded that students should be "judged on their hard work and merit, not prejudged by the color of their skin or their sex.”

EdTrust, a nonprofit that advocates for racial and economic equity in American education, said in a statement to K-12 Dive that the Trump administration is using civil rights protections meant to "dismantle discrimination and expand opportunity" to instead "undermine pathways into teaching for people historically excluded from the profession betrays that purpose."

“The administration’s false divide between ‘merit’ and diversity perpetuates the damaging suggestion that teachers of color are less qualified or less deserving," said Eric Duncan, director of Pre-K-12 policy of EdTrust, in the Nov. 16 statement. "It discounts their skills, expertise, and contributions while ignoring the systemic barriers that have denied talented people opportunities for generations. Scholarships that address those barriers help ensure that family wealth does not determine who can become a teacher."

The Education Department's investigations, Duncan said, "risk turning civil rights enforcement into a tool for preserving the very inequities it should confront.”

CUNY, which houses the NYC Men Teach Program being investigated by the department, said in a statement to K-12 Dive that it was "proud of its role educating the teachers of tomorrow and our programs are open to all students regardless of race, gender or national origin."

According to the city's website, the program was created around 2015 in collaboration with the federal Education Department and then-Mayor Bill de Blasio with the goal of putting an additional 1,000 men of color into the city’s teacher pipeline.

A March 2026 evaluation of the program conducted for the NYC mayor's office by Westat, which provides research services for government agencies, shows that the program contributed over 3,100 new male teachers of color to New York City Public Schools between school years 2015–16 and 2023–24.

The American Association of Colleges for Teacher Education said in a Nov. 16 email to K-12 Dive, “Strengthening and diversifying the educator pipeline is essential to addressing persistent teacher shortages and ensuring every PK-12 student has access to well-prepared, effective teachers. Decades of research confirm that a diverse educator workforce benefits all students academically, socially, and emotionally." AACTE added that it does not comment on active federal inquiries involving specific institutions or state agencies.

While the investigations are some of the first of their kind by OCR, they build on previous efforts by the Trump administration to weed out DEI in teacher preparation programs — especially in the wake of the U.S. Supreme Court’s 2023 ruling in Students for Fair Admissions v. Harvard.

Some of the earliest federal grants terminated in 2025 by the administration were under the Supporting Effective Educator Development Grant Program, the Teacher Quality Partnership Program and the Teacher and School Leader Incentive Program, due to what it said was the promotion of "divisive ideologies". The department admitted in a March court filing that it had cancelled 104 out of 109 active TQP and SEED grants.

The loss of over 100 federal teacher training grants last year caused at least 127 teacher candidates to drop out of their programs and another 259 accepted candidates to not enroll after all, according to survey findings released in October.

Those cuts were preceded by an anti-DEI directive that has since been heavily litigated and was most recently blocked in court in September.

That anti-DEI letter specifically called for agency personnel to review all new grant awards and issued grants to ensure those federal dollars “do not fund discriminatory practices — including in the form of DEI.” The directive added that “Illegal DEI policies and practices can violate both the letter and purpose of Federal civil rights law and conflict with the Department’s policy of prioritizing merit, fairness, and excellence in education.”

The Justice Department in August 2025 also warned districts they could be exposed to legal liability by asking job applicants how their “cultural background informs their teaching,” using recruitment strategies targeting candidates from specific geographic areas or racial backgrounds, and asking job candidates to describe how they overcame obstacles.

Since the Supreme Court’s decision in Students for Fair Admissions v. Harvard, civil rights groups have raised concerns that it would set back educational equity efforts even outside of race-conscious admissions. That decision undid race-conscious policies in college admissions, and the Education Department under Trump has used it to justify scaling back other diversity, equity and inclusion initiatives in education.

Scholarship availability, teacher pipelines and student affinity groups were among the top areas beyond college access that advocates were concerned could be impacted in the wake of the ruling.

Consider potential for streaming, content creation and community engagement, alongside development team capabilities, budgets, schedules, commercial potential and key risks.

Drive evaluation and signing by reviewing pitches, prototypes and playable builds, producing structured assessments and presenting recommendations internally. Build the scouting function by establishing processes, evaluation criteria and pipeline management. Monitor market trends and emerging opportunities, report on potential signings, and help recruit and develop the scouting team as the business grows.

Significant experience in PC/console publishing, scouting, business development or a related commercial role, with an established independent developer and studio network and a track record of identifying and progressing promising games.

Excellent knowledge of PC and console markets, genres, competitors and player expectations, alongside practical Steam experience, including publishing, launching games and building audiences.

Strong creative and commercial judgement, with the ability to evaluate gameplay, market potential, development capability and financial viability.

The confidence to make independent recommendations, challenge assumptions and communicate a compelling commercial rationale, supported by excellent relationship-building, presentation and cross-functional collaboration skills.

#7192. DHS’ 4-year cap on student visa stays struck down by federal judge

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The law does not grant the agency “such broad and open-ended authority to undermine” the student visa program, the judge wrote.

DHS published the rule in July, arguing it was needed to protect national security and prevent visa fraud. International students would have been able to seek extensions on their visas, but the decision would have been at federal officials’ sole discretion.

The rule would have changed long-standing policy permitting foreign students to stay in the U.S. for as long as it takes to complete their academic studies.

Several higher ed groups and unions, including the Presidents’ Alliance on Higher Education and Immigration and the American Federation of Teachers, sued the agency in August over the regulations.

Saylor handed the plaintiffs an early win when he temporarily blocked the visa cap from taking effect. Writing that they were likely to succeed in their case, the judge flagged that the rule could be abused by the Trump administration.

“It is not difficult to imagine how that system might be abused to punish those whose viewpoints or research are disfavored by the officials making the decision — or, indeed, punish disfavored institutions,” Saylor said in his Sept. 14 order.

He repeated those concerns Monday, describing the student visa process DHS sought to set up as an “unreviewable, completely discretionary renewal system.”

Saylor further said DHS failed to weigh “less burdensome” options.

The DHS rule would have done more than limit student visa stays. Some international students would have been barred from changing their field of study or transferring colleges. And they could not come to the U.S. to pursue an academic degree at the same or a lower level than a degree they already possess.

For example, an international student who holds a law degree would effectively be banned from pursuing a master’s degree in the U.S., Saylor said.

“Put bluntly, DHS is seeking to prevent or discourage the very activity — providing educational opportunities for foreign students in the United States — that Congress sought to permit and encourage,” he said. “The statute simply does not grant DHS such broad and open-ended authority” to undermine the student visa program.

The judge’s decision may not put the case to bed, as the Trump administration could still appeal it. The continued uncertainty comes as new data shows a decline in international enrollment in the U.S.

In 2025-26, the number of foreign students attending U.S. colleges dropped 2.7% year over year, according to the latest Open Doors report. And preliminary data from fall 2026 found that international enrollment declined another 9% annually at surveyed institutions. Both decreases were driven by losses of new students and those pursuing graduate degrees.

Get the free daily newsletter read by industry experts

Colleges would be on the hook for up to $100,000 in fees for each international student seeking to participate in Optional Practical Training.

Senior leaders attributed its financial woes to “shifting enrollment trends, unpredictable state and federal funding, and rising costs.”

You’ll work closely with Finance, Engineering, Legal, and our vendor partners.

This is a lead role for the Procurement function. The operating model, the source of truth for vendor spend, and the standards are yours to define, against a clear position: this is a FinOps role first, not a pure procurement desk. You'll own the technical and analytical side of vendor management, reporting, efficiency, and the commercial interface across the company, while keeping procurement governance tight. It's ideal for someone fluent across finance and engineering, who thrives in async, fast-moving environments, and wants to build a function from the ground up rather than inherit one.

Own vendor relationships: run our primary cloud and SaaS vendor relationships end-to-end (AWS, GCP, Cloudflare, CloudQuery, ClickHouse, and more), leading renewals and negotiations alongside Finance and Legal.

Run procurement governance: enforce our competitive-bid process for incoming requests and manage larger procurement through our managed-procurement partner, keeping oversight and accountability with FinOps.

People management: Build a healthy, high-trust team culture in a remote-first, globally distributed environment. Run 1:1s, performance management, career development, and onboarding for your direct report(s).Handle people admin (leave, time off, etc.) for your team.

Manage team’s delivery and accountability: Own the team's roadmap and project management (Linear), driving accountability and clear milestones. Manage workflow intake and Slack triage. Own the team's weekly updates and keep stakeholders aligned without creating overhead.

Drive commercial VIA: own spend visibility and unit economics, surface licensing and usage inefficiency before it becomes a surprise, and drive renewals, right-sizing, and tooling consolidation to realized savings.

Build reporting and governance: vendor and SaaS cost reporting is the unowned gap today and your highest-leverage area in the first year. Build the dashboards and reporting hands-on, partner with our data teams to scale them, and own the governance layer (spend policies, thresholds, accountability).

Coordinate the cloud efficiency program: keep optimization initiatives visible and tracked from commitment to realized savings, partnering with Engineering without taking over their delivery.

Have 8+ years in vendor/commercial management, procurement, FinOps, or technical/financial analysis, at a senior, function-owning level.

Have owned vendor relationships, contract negotiations, and renewals, and treat them as a lever on unit economics.

Have run procurement governance such as competitive-bid processes, benchmark analysis, approval thresholds, or managed-procurement partners.

Have high judgment and can hold a position and drive alignment without positional authority.

Build cost dashboards and reporting hands-on (SQL, BI tools), and are comfortable with vendor and cloud spend data (AWS, GCP, SaaS billing) and licensing/usage analysis.

Communicate clearly across both technical and non-technical audiences.

Have experience in async or globally distributed teams.

Are energized by building a function from the ground up and navigating ambiguity.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.

Atticus makes it easy for any sick or injured American to get life-changing aid. Our mission is to tear down barriers between people in crisis and the aid they deserve. So far, we’ve helped hundreds of thousands of people access over $10 billion in life-changing aid —and earned over 20,000 five-star reviews. And we’re just getting started.

Make contact with a client that’s in danger of slipping through the cracks at SSA, and help get their case back on track.

Work with a client’s team to run down missing paperwork at SSA.

Take on an inefficient workflow that’s slowing the team down (e.g., processing case updates), scope an improvement, get team buy-in, and execute the improvement.

This role is a rare opportunity to join a fast-growing Series C startup and do meaningful social impact work. It’s designed to be a path to building expertise in the fields of law you support and taking on greater responsibilities over time (e.g. becoming a paralegal).

You have a Bachelor’s degree from a four-year university with strong academic performance.

You have professional experience helping others apply for government benefits.

You have experience at a startup or other fast-paced, agile environment.

We are strongly committed to building a diverse team. If you’re from a background that’s underrepresented in tech, we’d love to meet you.

This is a rare opportunity to join a startup that has strong traction (substantial funding, well-respected backers, tremendous growth, and many happy customers) but is still small enough that you can have a huge impact and play a role in shaping our culture.

We’re a certified B Corporation tackling a critical social problem. Our mission to help people in need drives everything we do, and your work here will touch many lives.

Medical and dental insurance with 100% of employees and their families premiums covered

15 vacation days & ~20 paid holidays each year (including two weeks at the end-of-year)

$1,000/year reimbursable stipend for education and training outside of work

Up to $1,200/year student loan repayment assistance

Humble, thoughtful, smart, fun colleagues
